   \"content\": \"Command: /bin/bash -lc \\\"sed -n '1,260p' methods.txt\\\"\\nChunk ID: a7afc6\\nWall time: 0.0000 seconds\\nProcess exited with code 0\\nOriginal token count: 4597\\nOutput:\\nTask-switching behavioral paradigm\\n\\nWe designed a behavioral paradigm associated with multiple cognitive processes. In this paradigm, head-fixed mice performed two directional licking tasks that alternated block-wise within each behavioral session. These tasks varied in their cognitive demands but required the same instructed motor output.\\n\\nThis paradigm included an established delayed-response (DR) task in which motor planning is temporally dissociated from movement execution (Fig. 1a). In the DR task, an auditory stimulus indicated the location of a reward. After a brief delay epoch with no auditory stimuli, a separate auditory \\u2018go cue\\u2019 instructed the mouse to move. If the mouse performed a directional tongue movement to the correct target, a water reward was delivered. In separate blocks of trials, mice performed a water-cued (WC) task in which all auditory stimuli were omitted. Instead, a drop of water was presented at a random point in time at a randomly selected reward port. Animals consumed water upon its presentation. Mice received no explicit cues signaling DR and WC blocks. The DR task required a perceptual decision that guides the planning of a subsequent motor action. In the WC task, uncertainty in the timing and location of reward prevented formation of a motor plan before reward presentation, and, instead, mice detected reward availability using olfactory and/or vibrissal cues. This structure required animals to maintain an internal representation of the current block identity (context) to maintain high task performance.\\n\\nWe examined uninstructed movements in this behavioral paradigm using high-speed video. We tracked movement of the tongue, jaw, nose and paws and calculated instantaneous motion energy to quantify movement in a feature-agnostic manner (Fig. 1d\\u2013f). We found that mice performed uninstructed movements that varied in their identity and timing across both trials and contexts (Fig. 1e and Supplementary Movie 1).\\n\\nTo assess cortical involvement in instructed movements (directional licking) in both behavioral tasks, we used optogenetic photoinactivation to inhibit the ALM and tjM1 at the go cue (DR) or water presentation (WC) bilaterally in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice (10 sessions, four mice). Simultaneous photoinactivation of both regions at the go cue (DR task) or water drop (WC task) impaired the initiation of movement in both contexts (Fig. 1b,c; all DR trials: 41\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200920% reduction, mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d., P\\u2009=\\u20091\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u22124; all WC trials: 15\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200920%, P\\u2009=\\u20090.04; and Extended Data Fig. 1c,d). To assess cortical involvement in the expression of uninstructed movements, and whether these movements might be controlled by ALM or tjM1 preferentially, we bilaterally silenced the ALM or tjM1 individually during the delay epoch of DR trials (ALM: 15 sessions, tjM1: nine sessions, four mice). Uninstructed movements were suppressed by photoinactivation of either area (Extended Data Fig. 1b; ALM: 53\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200931% reduction, mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d., P\\u2009=\\u20093\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u22124; tjM1: 62\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200924% reduction, P\\u2009=\\u20092\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u22123, paired t-test) and more strongly suppressed by concurrent photoinactivation of both areas (Fig. 1g,h; 29 sessions, four mice; 73\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200935% reduction, mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d., P\\u2009=\\u20097\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u22129, paired t-test), similar to previous observations21 (but see ref. 34). In the DR task, delay photoinactivation of both areas led to a significant decrease in overall performance (Fig. 1i; 22%\\u2009\\u00b1\\u200914% reduction, mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d., P\\u2009=\\u20092\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u22126 paired t-test; Extended Data Fig. 1b) as expected from the disruption of choice/planning dynamics, often leading to animals responding consistently to a preferred side rather than in a manner commensurate with the sensory cue. These results indicate that both the ALM and tjM1 are involved in the generation of uninstructed movements and the planning and execution of instructed directional tongue movements in the two-context paradigm.\\n\\nNeural dynamics related to task variables\\n\\nWhile movement-related and task-related information is encoded in both the ALM and tjM1 in the DR task and other directional licking tasks33, task variables are more strongly represented in the ALM33,36, and so we focused our subsequent analyses there. We recorded activity extracellularly in the ALM with high-density silicon probes during the two-context paradigm to track dynamics associated with planning, context and the execution of movements (Fig. 2; two-context paradigm: 12 sessions, six mice, 522 units, including 214 well-isolated single units; an additional three mice were trained only on the DR task: 25 sessions, nine mice, 1,651 units, including 483 well-isolated single units; see Extended Data Fig. 2a for per-session statistics). In the DR task, we found that choice selectivity (right versus left DR trials) was widespread across all task epochs as expected (sample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units; 25 sessions, nine mice). Individual units showed a variety of activity patterns across trial types, including delay epoch preparatory activity, which has often been taken to be a neural signature of motor planning32,37 (Fig. 2b, left). Individual units were also selective for behavioral context during all task epochs, including during the inter-trial interval (ITI) (39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units), suggesting persistent coding of context (Fig. 2b, right).\\n\\nMethods\\n\\nMouse behavior\\n\\nBehavioral training was conducted using custom scripts written for Bpod r0.5 (https://www.sanworks.io/shop/products.php?productFamily=bpod). All mice used in experiments were first trained on the DR task with a fixed delay epoch until they reached at least 70% accuracy. At the beginning of each trial, one of two auditory tones lasting 1.3\\u2009s was played; the tone indicating a \\u2018right\\u2019 trial was white noise, and the tone indicating a \\u2018left\\u2019 trial was a pure tone (8-kHz pulses). The delay epoch (0.9\\u2009s for 12 mice, 0.7\\u2009s for one mouse and linearly time warped to 0.9\\u2009s) started after the completion of the sample tone. After the delay epoch, an auditory go cue was played (swept-frequency cosine (chirp), 10\\u2009ms), after which animals were rewarded with approximately 3\\u2009\\u00b5l of water for contacting the correct lickport. Lickport contacts before the response epoch resulted in the current task epoch restarting, ensuring that they could not progress to the next task epoch until they had stopped licking for the specified length of the current epoch. If the animal did not respond within 3\\u2009s of the go cue, this was considered an \\u2018ignore\\u2019 trial, although responses were typically registered within 300\\u2009ms of the go cue.\\n\\nMice used for the two-context task (Figs. 1, 2, 4 and 8 and Extended Data Figs. 1\\u20133 and 9) were introduced to the WC context after they were fully trained on the DR context and at least 2\\u2009d before the first electrophysiology recording session. A behavioral session began with approximately 100 DR trials and was then followed by alternating blocks of WC and DR trials. Each interleaved block was 10\\u201325 trials, with no fixed block duration. All sessions started with DR trials. Pilot sessions beginning with WC trials had to be terminated early due to a high \\u2018ignore\\u2019 rate, likely due to the mouse becoming sated early in the session. In a WC trial, all auditory cues were omitted, and an approximately 3-\\u00b5l water reward was presented randomly from either lickport. Trials in which the animal contacted the lickport before the reward (\\u2018early lick\\u2019) were omitted from analyses. ITIs were randomly drawn from an exponential distribution with mean 1.5\\u2009s. There were no explicit cues during the ITI to indicate to the animal which context block it was in.\\n\\nFor the randomized delay task (Fig. 3g,h,i), mice were first fully trained on the fixed delay DR task (delay duration of 0.9\\u2009s). The delay epoch length was then randomized; the duration for each trial was randomly selected from six possible values (0.3\\u2009s, 0.6\\u2009s, 1.2\\u2009s, 1.8\\u2009s, 2.4\\u2009s and 3.6\\u2009s). The probability of each delay duration being selected was assigned such that it resembled a probability density function of an exponential distribution with \\u03c4\\u2009=\\u20090.9\\u2009s, as in ref. 44. Animals were trained on this version of the task until they became experts (>70% accuracy and <20% early lick rate).\\n\\nVideography analysis\\n\\nHigh-speed video was captured (400-Hz frame rate) from two cameras (FLIR, Blackfly monochrome camera, BFS-U3-16S2M-CS). One provided a side view of the mouse, and the other provided a bottom view. We tracked the movements of specific body features using DeepLabCut (Figs. 1, 3 and 4). The tongue, jaw and nose were tracked using both cameras, whereas the paws were tracked using only the bottom view. Position and velocity of each tracked feature was calculated from each camera. The x and y position of each kinematic feature was extracted from the output of DeepLabCut. Missing values were filled in with the nearest available value for all features, except for the tongue. The velocity of each feature was then calculated as the first-order derivative of the position vector. Tongue angle and length were found using the bottom camera. Tongue angle was defined as the angle between the vector pointing from the jaw to the tip of the tongue and the vector defining the direction the mouse was facing. Tongue length was calculated as the Euclidean distance from the jaw to the tip of the tongue.\\n\\nMotion energy\\n\\nThe motion energy for a given frame and pixel was defined as the absolute value of the difference between the median value of the pixel across the next five frames (12.5\\u2009ms) and the median value of the pixel across the previous five frames (12.5\\u2009ms). Motion energy for each frame was then converted to a single value by taking the 99th percentile (~700\\u2009pixels) of motion energy values across the frame. Motion energy calculated in this manner was high during overt movements over small regions of pixel space, such as during whisking, while remaining relatively low during passive respiration that corresponded to subtle motion across the animal\\u2019s body. A threshold above which an animal was classed as moving was defined on a per-session basis manually. Motion energy distributions, per session, were bimodal, showing a large peak with little variance at low motion energy values and a second, smaller peak with high variance at large values. The threshold was set as the motion energy value separating these two modes. We found that this method of setting the threshold captured both short-duration and long-duration movements. Alternative methods, such as defining a baseline period of no movement against which to compare, were less successful due to the variability in timing of the movements across trials.\\n\\nPhotoinactivation experiments\\n\\nOptogenetic photoinactivation was deployed on approximately 30% of trials selected at random. A \\u2018masking flash\\u2019 (470-mm LEDs; LUXEON Rebel LED) controlled by an Arduino Teensy microcontroller (100-ms pulses at 10\\u2009Hz) was delivered constantly for the duration of the session to prevent mice from differentiating control and photoinactivation trials. A 488-nm laser (Coherent, OBIS 488-nm LX 150-mW laser system) was used for all photoinactivation experiments. ChR2-assisted photoinactivation (Fig. 1b,c,g and Extended Data Fig. 1b\\u2013d) was performed through a cranial window (see \\u2018Surgical procedures\\u2019 subsection), which replaced bone over the frontal cortex. Light was delivered either at the start of the delay epoch or at the start of the response epoch (only one epoch tested per session). A power density of 1.5\\u2009mW\\u2009mm\\u22122 was used for all photoinactivation experiments.\\n\\nFor delay epoch photoinactivation (Fig. 1g\\u2013i and Extended Data Fig. 1b), light was delivered at the onset of the delay epoch and lasted for 0.6\\u2009s, followed by a 0.2-s linear ramp down. We targeted one of three regions per session: bilateral motor cortex (ALM and tjM1), bilateral ALM or bilateral tjM1. A scanning galvo system (Thorlabs, GPS011) was used to simultaneously target both hemispheres by scanning at 40\\u2009Hz. The beam (2-mm diameter) was centered around the following locations: ALM (AP 2.5\\u2009mm, ML 1.5\\u2009mm), tjM1 (AP 1.75\\u2009mm, ML 2.25\\u2009mm) and motor cortex (that is, ALM and tjM1; AP 2.25\\u2009mm, ML 1.85\\u2009mm). Due to their proximity, when specifically targeting ALM or tjM1, Kwik-Cast (World Precision Instruments) was applied to the surrounding regions to prevent photoinactivation of other regions. For photoinactivation at the go cue or water drop (Fig. 1b,c and Extended Data Fig. 1c,d), light was delivered to the motor cortex for 0.8\\u2009s, followed by a 0.2-s ramp down beginning at the go cue/water drop presentation.\\n\\n\\nElectrophysiology recordings\\n\\nExtracellular recordings were performed in the ALM using one of two types of silicon probes: H2 probes, which have two shanks, each with 32 channels with 25-\\u00b5m spacing (Cambridge Neurotech), or Neuropixels 1.0 (ref. 59), which have one shank and allow recording from 384 channels arranged in a checkerboard pattern (IMEC). For recordings with H2 probes, the 64-channel voltage signals were multiplexed using a Whisper recording system (Neural Circuits, LLC), recorded on a PXI-8133 board (National Instruments) and digitized at 14 bits. The signals were demultiplexed into 64 (for H2 probes) or 384 (for Neuropixels 1.0) voltage traces sampled at 25\\u2009kHz and stored for offline analysis.\\n\\nAt least 6\\u2009h before recording, a small craniotomy (1\\u20131.5-mm diameter) was made over the ALM (AP 2.5\\u2009mm, ML 1.5\\u2009mm). Then, 2\\u20135 recordings were performed on a single craniotomy on consecutive days. After inserting the probes between 900\\u2009\\u00b5m and 1,100\\u2009\\u00b5m (New Scale Technologies, MPM System), brain tissue was allowed to settle for at least 5\\u2009min before starting recordings. All recordings were made using SpikeGLX (https://github.com/billkarsh/SpikeGLX, phase3a release).\\n\\nBehavioral analysis\\n\\nBehavioral data were collected using Bpod r0.5 (https://www.sanworks.io/shop/products.php?productFamily=bpod). All sessions used for behavioral analysis had at least 40 correct DR trials for each direction (left or right) and 20 correct WC trials for each direction, excluding early lick and ignore trials, which were omitted from all analyses. To assess the impact of ALM photoinactivation on movement initiation, we first calculated the percent of trials with a correct lick within 600\\u2009ms of the go cue/water drop (Fig. 1c). To find the fraction of time with the tongue visible during the photoinactivation period (Extended Data Fig. 1d), for each trial we found the number of timepoints within the 1\\u2009s after the go cue/water drop where the tongue was labeled as visible by DeepLabCut and divided that by the total number of timepoints within the photoinactivation period. This was then averaged for all control or photoinactivation trials for a given session.\\n\\nTo assess the impact of bilateral motor cortex/ALM/tjM1 photoinactivation during the delay epoch, the average motion energy during the delay epoch was calculated separately for right and left control versus photoinactivation trials (Fig. 1h and Extended Data Fig. 1b).\\n\\nElectrophysiology recording analysis\\n\\nJRCLUST (https://github.com/JaneliaSciComp/JRCLUST, version 4.1.0) and/or Kilosort 3 (ref. 61) (https://github.com/MouseLand/Kilosort) with manual curation using Phy 2 (https://github.com/cortex-lab/phy) were used for spike sorting. A unit was considered a well-isolated single unit based on manual inspection of its inter-spike interval (ISI) histogram, separation from other units and its stationarity across the session. Units that passed manual curation but had a higher ISI violation rate were called multiunits. Recording sessions were included for analysis only if they had at least 10 units (see Extended Data Fig. 2 for a distribution of neuron counts across sessions). For the DR task, we recorded 1,651 units (483 single units) in ALM from 25 sessions using nine mice. In 12 sessions from six mice, animals performed the two-context task. In total, 522 units (214 well-isolated single units) were recorded in these sessions. Finally, for the randomized delay task, we recorded 845 units (288 well-isolated single units) in ALM from 19 sessions using four mice. For subspace alignment (Figs. 5g,n, 7d,h and 8d) and single-unit selectivity analyses (Fig. 3g and Extended Data Fig. 4c), only well-isolated single units with firing rates exceeding 1\\u2009Hz were included. All units with firing rates exceeding 1\\u2009Hz were included in all other analyses.\\n\\nTo find choice-selective neurons, 40 trials were subsampled for both right and left correct trials, and a rank-sum test was used to compare spike counts for each unit during the sample, delay or response epochs. Selective units were those with significantly different spike counts (P\\u2009<\\u20090.05) during a given epoch. Context-selective units were defined in a two-step process to ensure that we were not conflating context selectivity with slow changes in animal engagement/motivation over each session. First, 40 trials each were subsampled for DR and WC trials, and spike counts were compared (P\\u2009<\\u20090.05, rank-sum test) during the ITI (the 300\\u2009ms preceding the sample tone onset) when the animal received no external cues to indicate which context it was in. Because sessions always began with a DR block and often ended with a WC block, it is possible that differences in firing rates across contexts defined in this way could be representing time within the session. To account for this, for each unit identified in the first step, we calculated the difference in spike rate for each pair of DR and WC blocks (for example, a session with five blocks, DR\\u2013WC\\u2013DR\\u2013WC\\u2013DR, would have nPairs\\u2009=\\u20094 adjacent, overlapping block pairs) and included units as context selective only if their preferred context (context with a higher spike rate) was the same for at least nPairs\\u2009\\u2212\\u20091 of the block pairs.\\n\\nSelectivity in the neural population (Extended Data Fig. 4c) was calculated as the difference in spike rate on preferred\\u2009\\u2212\\u2009non-preferred trials in choice-selective units, with preferred trials referring to the trial type with a higher spike rate for each unit.\\n\"\n3545:            \"cmd\": \"python3 - We recorded activity extracellularly in the ALM with high-density silicon probes during the two-context paradigm to track dynamics associated with planning, context and the execution of movements (Fig. 2; two-context paradigm: 12 sessions, six mice, 522 units, including 214 well-isolated single units; an additional three mice were trained only on the DR task: 25 sessions, nine mice, 1,651 units, including 483 well-isolated single units; see Extended Data Fig. 2a for per-session statistics). In the DR task, we found that choice selectivity (right versus left DR trials) was widespread across all task epochs as expected (sample: 36%; delay: 42%; response: 58% of 483 single units; 25 sessions, nine mice). Individual units showed a variety of activity patterns across trial types, including delay epoch preparatory activity, which has often been taken to be a neural signature of motor planning32,37 (Fig. 2b, left). Individual units were also selective for behavioral context during all task epochs, including during the inter-trial interval (ITI) (39% of single units, 12 sessions, six mice, 214 single units), suggesting persistent coding of context (Fig. 2b, right).\\n19:Behavioral training was conducted using custom scripts written for Bpod r0.5 (https://www.sanworks.io/shop/products.php?productFamily=bpod). All mice used in experiments were first trained on the DR task with a fixed delay epoch until they reached at least 70% accuracy. At the beginning of each trial, one of two auditory tones lasting 1.3\\u2009s was played; the tone indicating a \\u2018right\\u2019 trial was white noise, and the tone indicating a \\u2018left\\u2019 trial was a pure tone (8-kHz pulses). The delay epoch (0.9\\u2009s for 12 mice, 0.7\\u2009s for one mouse and linearly time warped to 0.9\\u2009s) started after the completion of the sample tone. After the delay epoch, an auditory go cue was played (swept-frequency cosine (chirp), 10\\u2009ms), after which animals were rewarded with approximately 3\\u2009\\u00b5l of water for contacting the correct lickport. Lickport contacts before the response epoch resulted in the current task epoch restarting, ensuring that they could not progress to the next task epoch until they had stopped licking for the specified length of the current epoch. If the animal did not respond within 3\\u2009s of the go cue, this was considered an \\u2018ignore\\u2019 trial, although responses were typically registered within 300\\u2009ms of the go cue.\\n21:Mice used for the two-context task (Figs. 1, 2, 4 and 8 and Extended Data Figs. 1\\u20133 and 9) were introduced to the WC context after they were fully trained on the DR context and at least 2\\u2009d before the first electrophysiology recording session. A behavioral session began with approximately 100 DR trials and was then followed by alternating blocks of WC and DR trials. Each interleaved block was 10\\u201325 trials, with no fixed block duration. All sessions started with DR trials. Pilot sessions beginning with WC trials had to be terminated early due to a high \\u2018ignore\\u2019 rate, likely due to the mouse becoming sated early in the session. In a WC trial, all auditory cues were omitted, and an approximately 3-\\u00b5l water reward was presented randomly from either lickport. We again trained a multiple linear regression \\nmodel to predict single-trial projections along CDcontext from kinematic \\nfeatures and found that, like putative choice and urgency dynamics, \\ncontext-selective signals were associated with context-specific move-\\nments (Fig. 4c,d; R2\\u2009=\\u20090.49\\u2009\\u00b1\\u20090.22, mean\\u2009\\u00b1\\u2009s.d across sessions, minimum \\nR2\\u2009=\\u20090.097, maximum R2\\u2009=\\u20090.751, n\\u2009=\\u200912 sessions).\\nTaken together, these findings suggest that dynamics along \\nCDchoice, CDramp and CD context could be associated with uninstructed \\nmovements and/or the cognitive processes related to the anticipa -\\ntion and planning of upcoming instructed movements and encoding \\nof task context.\\nSubspace decomposition of trial-averaged neural activity\\nWe next examined whether neural signals encoding task variables could \\nbe isolated from movement-related signals, despite their correlation \\nin time. We hypothesized that, if the neural dynamics associated with \\ncognitive and motor processes are separable, their components in the \\nALM should be driven by distinct latent signals.\\nPrevious theoretical work demonstrated a simple mechanism \\nthat explains how neural activity can vary dynamically while ensur -\\ning that specific variables remain stably encoded 28. In this formal -\\nism, a variable of interest may be decoded from a neural population, \\nperhaps by a downstream circuit, through a linear transformation \\nmatrix, W, which could be taken to follow from a particular pattern of \\nsynaptic connectivity. If the dimensionality of neural activity is larger \\nthan the dimensionality of the output signals decoded (that is, if W is  \\nnot square), then W  must have a null space\\u2014dimensions in activity \\nspace along which neural activity can vary without changing the \\ndecoded output28.\\nApplying this idea to recordings from the primate motor cortex \\ndemonstrated that motor planning dynamics are confined to the null \\nspace of a linear transformation between motor cortical activity and \\nmuscle activation25, thereby allowing motor planning signals to evolve \\ndynamically in a manner that is independent of motor output. The \\northogonal complement to this output-null subspace was termed \\nthe output-potent subspace; changes in activity along output-potent \\ndimensions were then associated with changes in muscle activation25.\\nSubsequent work identified analogous subspaces of neural \\nactivity in the primate motor cortex as the orthogonal subspaces \\nthat capture the most variance in neural activity during the delay and \\nresponse epochs of a delayed-response task to capture preparatory \\nand movement-related activity, respectively27. This approach is effec-\\ntive when preparatory and movement-related dynamics are confined \\nto distinct temporal epochs and when neural activity is exclusively \\nrelated to motor preparation and motor execution, respectively, in \\nthose epochs. Conveniently, it avoids the need to measure muscle \\nactivation or any other descriptor of motor output. It also avoids the \\nneed to explicitly model the transformation between neural activity \\nand motor output with a linear (or any other parametric) transforma-\\ntion. This transformation is generally unknown and can be highly \\nnonlinear, particularly for movements directly controlled by central \\npattern generators, such as those that support much of the behavioral \\nrepertoire of rodents (for example, locom\\n\\n--- PAGE 6 ---\\nNature Neuroscience | Volume 28 | March 2025 | 640\\u2013653\\n 645\\nArticle https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-024-01859-1\\nmodified this analytical approach in two ways (see Supplementary \\nNote 1 for a more detailed discussion). Because uninstructed move -\\nments vary considerably from trial to trial, we avoided analyses of \\ntrial-averaged data and focused on single-trial data. Second, rather \\nthan identifying subspaces capturing variance during the delay \\n(when no movement is assumed) and response epochs, we instead \\nannotated all timepoints across a session in which animals were mov-\\ning and all timepoints at which animals were stationary. This could \\nbe straightforwardly achieved using a threshold on motion energy \\n(the magnitude of frame-by-frame changes in video). We found two \\northogonal subspaces that best explained variance during all time -\\npoints across a session at which animals were stationary and moving \\n(Fig. 5h). We termed the resulting subspaces \\u2018movement-null\\u2019 and \\n\\u2018movement-potent\\u2019 subspaces. The movement-potent subspace con-\\ntains dynamics associated with movement. Neural \\ndynamics in the movement-potent subspace were correlated with \\nmotion energy on single trials, as might be expected (Fig. 5i,l,m). \\nNaively, movement-null subspace activity might be expected to be \\nsimilarly anticorrelated with motion energy because that subspace is \\nconstructed to capture variance during periods of animal stationarity. \\nThis outcome was largely not observed. Rather, movement-null sub-\\nspace activity did not display any consistent relationship with move-\\nment (Fig. 5i,l,m), suggesting that these \\u2018internal\\u2019 dynamics were not \\nonly prominent in the absence of movement but also persisted during \\nmovement, in line with the supposition that cognitive processes can \\npersist during movement.\\nUninstructed movements were commonly observed during \\nthe delay epoch of DR trials and were highly correlated with puta -\\ntive choice and urgency signals (Fig. 3c\\u2013h). T o examine whether the \\nmovement-potent subspace might inadvertently capture choice or \\nurgency dynamics that are correlated in time with movements, we \\nestimated subspaces using trials from the WC context only. Here, \\nthe movement-potent subspace is determined in a context in which \\nchoice and urgency signals are absent 13 (Fig. 2d and Extended Data \\nFig. 4c), precluding the possibility that dimensions of neural activ -\\nity related to choice and urgency are inadvertently assigned to the \\nmovement-potent subspace due to their correlation with movement. \\nThis approach yielded subspaces that were highly similar (Extended \\nData Fig. 4d\\u2013f). In another control, examining movement-null and \\nmovement-potent subspaces estimated only using data recorded \\nduring the response epoch of both tasks, when planning and urgency \\ndynamics should be minimal, again yielded similar results (Extended \\nData Fig. 4g\\u2013i). Finally, we estimated the movement-null subspace as \\nthe subspace defined by the top principal components (PCs) of activ-\\nity recorded during periods of stationarity and then determined the \\nmovement-potent subspace as the top PCs of dynamics not already \\ncaptured in the movement-null subspace. This latter procedure, which \\nis highly conservative in avoiding the spurious assignment of internal \\ndynamics to the movement-potent subspace, again yielded similar \\nresults (Extended Data Fig. 4j\\u2013l and Methods). We also confirmed \\nthat the choice of subspace dimensionality had minimal impact on \\nthe dynamics contained within each (Extended Data Fig. 6). Thus, the \\nmovement-potent subspace captures movement-related signals and \\nnot simply dynamics related to internal processes that are correlated \\nin time with movements.\\nAnimals may think and move simultaneously. Thus, it follows \\nthat internal dynamics contained in the movement-null subspace may \\nbe present during the response epoch. Additionally, uninstructed \\nmovements occur during both the sample and delay epochs, and so \\nwe should observe dynamics within the movement-potent subspace \\nduring both task epochs. As expected, activity within movement-null \\nand movement-potent subspaces captured variance across epochs \\n(Fig. 5k; see also Supplementary Note 2). Interestingly, the propor -\\ntion of units with activity largely confined to the movement-potent \\nsubspace was substantially larger than expected by chance (Fig. 5n and \\nExtended Data Fig. 5b; P\\u2009=\\u20096\\u2009\\u00d7\\u200910\\u221219 , n\\u2009=\\u2009483 single units; Methods) and \\nlarger than when subspaces were identified from trial-averaged data \\nhere (Fig. 5g) and in non-human primates25. \\\"motion_energy\\\": motion_energy,\\n+        \\\"motion_thresh\\\": motion_thresh,\\n+    }\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_session_hdf5(spec: SessionSpec) -> dict:\\n+    with h5py.File(spec.session_path, \\\"r\\\") as f:\\n+        bp = f[\\\"obj/bp\\\"]\\n+        probe_groups = h5_nonempty_probe_groups(f, f[\\\"obj/clu\\\"])\\n+        probe = probe_groups[spec.probe - 1]\\n+        units = []\\n+        for i in range(probe[\\\"trialtm\\\"].shape[0]):\\n+            units.append(\\n+                {\\n+                    \\\"quality\\\": h5_read_string(f, probe[\\\"quality\\\"][i, 0]),\\n+                    \\\"trialtm\\\": np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, probe[\\\"trialtm\\\"][i, 0]), dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                    \\\"trial\\\": np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, probe[\\\"trial\\\"][i, 0]), dtype=np.int64).reshape(-1),\\n+                }\\n+            )\\n+\\n+        traj_views = []\\n+        needed_by_view = [[\\\"tongue\\\"], [\\\"top_paw\\\", \\\"bottom_paw\\\"]]\\n+        traj_refs = np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/traj\\\"][()])\\n+        for view_idx in range(2):\\n+            view_group = f[traj_refs[view_idx, 0]]\\n+            feat_indices = {}\\n+            for trial_idx in range(view_group[\\\"featNames\\\"].shape[0]):\\n+                feat_names = h5_read_string_list(f, view_group[\\\"featNames\\\"][trial_idx, 0])\\n+                for name in needed_by_view[view_idx]:\\n+                    if name not in feat_indices and name in feat_names:\\n+                        feat_indices[name] = feat_names.index(name)\\n+                if len(feat_indices) == len(needed_by_view[view_idx]):\\n+                    break\\n+\\n+            trials = []\\n+            for trial_idx in range(view_group[\\\"ts\\\"].shape[0]):\\n+                ts = np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, view_group[\\\"ts\\\"][trial_idx, 0]), dtype=np.float64)\\n+                if ts.ndim == 2:\\n+                    ts = ts[:, :, None]\\n+                frame_times = None\\n+                try:\\n+                    frame_times = np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, view_group[\\\"frameTimes\\\"][trial_idx, 0]), dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+                    if frame_times.size == 0:\\n+                        frame_times = None\\n+                except Exception:\\n+                    frame_times = None\\n+                try:\\n+                    ndropped = float(np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, view_group[\\\"NdroppedFrames\\\"][trial_idx, 0])).reshape(-1)[0])\\n+                except Exception:\\n+                    ndropped = float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n+\\n+                selected_names = []\\n+                selected_ts = []\\n+                for name in needed_by_view[view_idx]:\\n+                    if name in feat_indices and ts.size:\\n+                        selected_names.append(name)\\n+                        selected_ts.append(ts[:, :, feat_indices[name]])\\n+                if selected_ts:\\n+                    selected_ts = np.stack(selected_ts, axis=2)\\n+                else:\\n+                    selected_ts = np.empty((ts.shape[0], 3, 0), dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+                trials.append(\\n+                    {\\n+                        \\\"feat_names\\\": selected_names,\\n+                        \\\"ts\\\": selected_ts,\\n+                        \\\"frame_times\\\": frame_times,\\n+                        \\\"n_dropped\\\": ndropped,\\n+                    }\\n+                )\\n+            traj_views.append(trials)\\n+\\n+        me_path = spec.session_path.parent / f\\\"motionEnergy_{spec.subject}_{spec.date}.mat\\\"\\n+        motion_energy, motion_thresh = load_motion_energy(me_path)\\n+\\n+        bitstart = np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, f[\\\"obj/sglx/bitcode/bitstart\\\"]), dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+        fs = float(np.asarray(h5_read_numeric(f, f[\\\"obj/sglx/fs\\\"])).reshape(-1)[0])\\n+\\n+        return {\\n+            \\\"format\\\": \\\"hdf5\\\",\\n+            \\\"session_id\\\": spec.session_id,\\n+            \\\"subject\\\": spec.subject,\\n+            \\\"R\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"R\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"L\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"L\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"hit\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"hit\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"miss\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"miss\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"no\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"no\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"early\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"early\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"autowater\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"autowater\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"stim_enable\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/stim/enable\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"events\\\": {\\n+                \\\"bitStart\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/bitStart\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"sample\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/sample\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"delay\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/delay\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"goCue\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/goCue\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"reward\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/reward\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            },\\n+            \\\"units\\\": units,\\n+            \\\"traj\\\": traj_views,\\n+            \\\"bitcode_bitstart\\\": bitstart,\\n+            \\\"fs\\\": fs,\\n+            \\\"motion_energy\\\": motion_energy,\\n+            \\\"motion_thresh\\\": motion_thresh,\\n+        }\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_session(spec: SessionSpec) -> dict:\\n+    return load_session_hdf5(spec) if is_hdf5_mat(spec.session_path) else load_session_mat(spec)\\n+\\n+\\n+def good_quality(label: str) -> bool:\\n+    label = label.strip().lower()\\n+    return label not in {\\\"garbage\\\", \\\"gabrga\\\", \\\"noisy\\\", \\\"real?\\\"}\\n+\\n+\\n+def interp_to_taxis(old_t: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_t: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    old_t = np.asarray(old_t, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    mask = np.isfinite(old_t) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if mask.sum() < 2:\\n+        return np.full_like(new_t, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    return np.interp(new_t, old_t[mask], values[mask], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\\n+\\n+\\n+def find_video_offset(raw: dict) -> float:\\n+    bitstart = np.asarray(raw[\\\"bitcode_bitstart\\\"], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    fs = float(raw[\\\"fs\\\"])\\n+    if bitstart.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(fs) or fs <= 0:\\n+        return 0.5\\n+    return robust_mode(bitstart) / fs - robust_mode(raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"bitStart\\\"])\\n+\\n+\\n+def feature_xy(raw: dict, view_idx: int, feature_name: str, align_times: np.ndarray, time_vec: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\n+    trials = raw[\\\"traj\\\"][view_idx]\\n+    taxis = time_vec.copy()\\n+    vidshift = find_video_offset(raw)\\n+    xpos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    ypos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+    for trix, trial in enumerate(trials):\\n+        if not math.isfinite(trial[\\\"n_dropped\\\"]):\\n+            continue\\n+        if feature_name not in trial[\\\"feat_names\\\"]:\\n+            continue\\n+        feat_idx = trial[\\\"feat_names\\\"].index(feature_name)\\n+        ts = np.asarray(trial[\\\"ts\\\"][:, :2, feat_idx], dtype=np.float64)\\n+        if ts.ndim != 2 or ts.shape[0] == 0:\\n+            continue\\n+        frame_times = trial[\\\"frame_times\\\"]\\n+        if frame_times is None or frame_times.size == 0 or np.all(~np.isfinite(frame_times)):\\n+            frame_times = (np.arange(ts.shape[0], dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0\\n+        old_t = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trix]\\n+        xpos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 0], taxis)\\n+        ypos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 1], taxis)\\n+\\n+        if \\\"tongue\\\" not in feature_name:\\n+            xpos[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(xpos[:, trix])\\n+            ypos[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(ypos[:, trix])\\n+\\n+    return xpos, ypos\\n+\\n+\\n+def feature_speed(xpos: np.ndarray, ypos: np.ndarray, feature_name: str) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    n_time, n_trials = xpos.shape\\n+    xvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    yvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+    for i in range(n_trials):\\n+        tsinterp = np.column_stack([xpos[:, i], ypos[:, i]])\\n+        basederiv = np.nanmedian(np.diff(tsinterp, axis=0), axis=0) if n_time > 1 else np.array([0.0, 0.0])\\n+        xv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\\n+        yv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\\n+        if \\\"tongue\\\" not in feature_name:\\n+            xv = xv - basederiv[0]\\n+            yv = yv - basederiv[0]\\n+            xv = nearest_fill_1d(xv)\\n+            yv = nearest_fill_1d(yv)\\n+        else:\\n+            xv = np.nan_to_num(xv, nan=0.0)\\n+            yv = np.nan_to_num(yv, nan=0.0)\\n+        xvel[:, i] = xv\\n+        yvel[:, i] = yv\\n+    return np.sqrt(xvel**2 + yvel**2)\\n+\\n+\\n+def aligned_motion_energy(raw: dict, align_times: np.ndarray, time_vec: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    me_trials = raw[\\\"motion_energy\\\"]\\n+    side_trials = raw[\\\"traj\\\"][0]\\n+    vidshift = find_video_offset(raw)\\n+    out = np.full((time_vec.size, len(me_trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    for trix, me in enumerate(me_trials):\\n+        me = np.asarray(me, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+        if me.size == 0:\\n+            continue\\n+        frame_times = side_trials[trix][\\\"frame_times\\\"] if trix < len(side_trials) else None\\n+        if frame_times is None or frame_times.size == 0 or np.all(~np.isfinite(frame_times)):\\n+            old_t = (np.arange(me.size, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0 - 0.5 - align_times[trix]\\n+        else:\\n+            old_t = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trix]\\n+        out[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, me, time_vec)\\n+        out[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(out[:, trix])\\n+    return out\\n+\\n+\\n+def compute_unit_trial_matrix(unit: dict, go_cue: np.ndarray, trial_to_pos: np.ndarray, n_sel: int, time_edges: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    aligned = unit[\\\"trialtm\\\"] - go_cue[unit[\\\"trial\\\"] - 1]\\n+    trial_pos = trial_to_pos[unit[\\\"trial\\\"] - 1]\\n+    keep = (\\n+        (trial_pos >= 0)\\n+        & np.isfinite(aligned)\\n+        & (aligned >= time_edges[0])\\n+        & (aligned < time_edges[-1])\\n+    )\\n+    mat = np.zeros((n_sel, time_edges.size - 1), dtype=np.float64)\\n+    if np.any(keep):\\n+        bins = np.floor((aligned[keep] - time_edges[0]) / DT).astype(np.int64)\\n+        np.add.at(mat, (trial_pos[keep], bins), 1.0 / DT)\\n+    mat = my_smooth(mat.T, SMOOTH, BCTYPE).T\\n+    return mat\\n+\\n+\\n+def summarize_threshold(values: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    values = values[np.isfinite(values)]\\n+    if values.size == 0:\\n+        return 0.0\\n+    return float(np.nanpercentile(values, 50.0))\\n+\\n+\\n+def session_valid_trial_mask(raw: dict) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    return (\\n+        (raw[\\\"stim_enable\\\"] == 0)\\n+        & (raw[\\\"early\\\"] == 0)\\n+        & ((raw[\\\"hit\\\"] == 1) | (raw[\\\"miss\\\"] == 1))\\n+        & ((raw[\\\"R\\\"] == 1) | (raw[\\\"L\\\"] == 1))\\n+    )\\n+\\n+\\n+def plot_processing(session_id: str, time_vec: np.ndarray, session_out: dict, outdir: Path) -> None:\\n+    if not session_out[\\\"neural\\\"]:\\n+        return\\n+    trial_idx = 0\\n+    neural = session_out[\\\"neural\\\"][trial_idx]\\n+    cont = session_out[\\\"continuous\\\"]\\n+    binary = session_out[\\\"output\\\"][trial_idx]\\n+\\n+    fig, axes = plt.subplots(3, 2, figsize=(16, 11))\\n+    ax = axes[0, 0]\\n+    nshow = min(40, neural.shape[0])\\n+    ax.imshow(neural[:nshow], aspect=\\\"auto\\\", cmap=\\\"viridis\\\", extent=[time_vec[0], time_vec[-1], nshow, 0])\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Neural activity (sample trial)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylabel(\\\"Neuron\\\")\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[0, 1]\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, session_out[\\\"input\\\"][trial_idx][0], color=\\\"black\\\", lw=1.5)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Decoder input\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylabel(\\\"Seconds\\\")\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[1, 0]\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"tongue\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"tongue @ {cont['tongue_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"paw\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"paw @ {cont['paw_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"motion\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"motion @ {cont['motion_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Session thresholds\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[1, 1]\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"tongue\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"tongue\\\")\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"paw\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"paw\\\")\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"motion\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"motion\\\")\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Continuous outputs (sample trial)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[2, 0]\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[3], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"tongue_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[4], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"paw_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[5], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"motion_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylim(-0.2, 1.2)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Discretized continuous outputs\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[2, 1]\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[0], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"lick_direction\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[1], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"context\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[2], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"outcome\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylim(-0.2, 1.2)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Trial labels as time series\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    fig.suptitle(session_id)\\n+    fig.tight_layout()\\n+    fig.savefig(outdir / f\\\"processing_{session_id}.png\\\", dpi=150)\\n+    plt.close(fig)\\n+\\n+\\n+def convert_one_session(spec: SessionSpec, show_processing: bool, outdir: Path) -> dict | None:\\n+    t0 = time.time()\\n+    raw = load_session(spec)\\n+    valid = session_valid_trial_mask(raw)\\n+    selected_trials = np.flatnonzero(valid)\\n+    if selected_trials.size < 2:\\n+        log(f\\\"SKIP {spec.session_id}: only {selected_trials.size} valid hit/miss non-stim non-early trials\\\")\\n+        return None\\n+\\n+    time_edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    time_vec = time_edges[:-1] + DT / 2.0\\n+\\n+    trial_to_pos = np.full(raw[\\\"R\\\"].size, -1, dtype=np.int64)\\n+    trial_to_pos[selected_trials] = np.arange(selected_trials.size, dtype=np.int64)\\n+\\n+    kept_units = 0\\n+    neural_trials = [[] for _ in range(selected_trials.size)]\\n+    for unit in raw[\\\"units\\\"]:\\n+        if not good_quality(unit[\\\"quality\\\"]):\\n+            continue\\n+        unit_mat = compute_unit_trial_matrix(unit, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], trial_to_pos, selected_trials.size, time_edges)\\n+        if float(unit_mat.mean()) <= LOW_FR_HZ:\\n+            continue\\n+        kept_units += 1\\n+        unit_mat = unit_mat.astype(np.float32)\\n+        for trix in range(selected_trials.size):\\n+            neural_trials[trix].append(unit_mat[trix])\\n+\\n+    if kept_units < 10:\\n+        log(f\\\"SKIP {spec.session_id}: only {kept_units} units after quality/FR filtering\\\")\\n+        return None\\n+\\n+    tongue_pos = feature_xy(raw, 0, \\\"tongue\\\", raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+    tongue_speed = feature_speed(*tongue_pos, \\\"tongue\\\")\\n+\\n+    paw_speeds = []\\n+    for paw_name in (\\\"top_paw\\\", \\\"bottom_paw\\\"):\\n+        paw_pos = feature_xy(raw, 1, paw_name, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+        paw_speeds.append(feature_speed(*paw_pos, paw_name))\\n+    paw_speed = np.nanmean(np.stack(paw_speeds, axis=0), axis=0)\\n+    paw_speed = np.nan_to_num(paw_speed, nan=0.0)\\n+\\n+    motion = aligned_motion_energy(raw, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+    motion = np.nan_to_num(motion, nan=0.0)\\n+\\n+    tongue_sel = tongue_speed[:, selected_trials]\\n+    paw_sel = paw_speed[:, selected_trials]\\n+    motion_sel = motion[:, selected_trials]\\n+\\n+    tongue_thr = summarize_threshold(tongue_sel)\\n+    paw_thr = summarize_threshold(paw_sel)\\n+    motion_thr = summarize_threshold(motion_sel)\\n+\\n+    input_trials = []\\n+    output_trials = []\\n+    final_neural = []\\n+    for local_idx, trial_idx in enumerate(selected_trials):\\n+        neural_arr = np.stack(neural_trials[local_idx], axis=0).astype(np.float32)\\n+        final_neural.append(neural_arr)\\n+        input_trials.append(time_vec[None, :].astype(np.float32))\\n+\\n+        lick_direction = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"R\\\"][trial_idx] == 1 else 0)\\n+        context = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"autowater\\\"][trial_idx] == 0 else 0)\\n+        outcome = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"hit\\\"][trial_idx] == 1 else 0)\\n+        output_arr = np.vstack(\\n+            [\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, lick_direction, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, context, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, outcome, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                (tongue_sel[:, local_idx] >= tongue_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+                (paw_sel[:, local_idx] >= paw_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+                (motion_sel[:, local_idx] >= motion_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+            ]\\n+        )\\n+        output_trials.append(output_arr)\\n+\\n+    session_out = {\\n+        \\\"session_id\\\": spec.session_id,\\n+        \\\"subject\\\": spec.subject,\\n+        \\\"neural\\\": final_neural,\\n+        \\\"input\\\": input_trials,\\n+        \\\"output\\\": output_trials,\\n+        \\\"brain_region_idx\\\": np.zeros((kept_units,), dtype=np.int64),\\n+        \\\"n_trials\\\": len(final_neural),\\n+        \\\"n_units\\\": kept_units,\\n+        \\\"format\\\": raw[\\\"format\\\"],\\n+        \\\"source_file\\\": str(spec.session_path),\\n+        \\\"continuous\\\": {\\n+            \\\"tongue\\\": tongue_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"paw\\\": paw_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"motion\\\": motion_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"tongue_thr\\\": tongue_thr,\\n+            \\\"paw_thr\\\": paw_thr,\\n+            \\\"motion_thr\\\": motion_thr,\\n+        },\\n+    }\\n+    if show_processing:\\n+        plot_processing(spec.session_id, time_vec, session_out, outdir)\\n+\\n+    log(\\n+        f\\\"SESSION {spec.session_id}: kept {session_out['n_trials']} trials, \\\"\\n+        f\\\"{session_out['n_units']} units, format={raw['format']}, \\\"\\n+        f\\\"time={time.time() - t0:.2f}s\\\"\\n+    )\\n+    return session_out\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_dataset(session_specs: list[SessionSpec], show_processing: bool, outdir: Path) -> dict:\\n+    neural = []\\n+    inputs = []\\n+    outputs = []\\n+    subject_names: list[str] = []\\n+    subject_index = []\\n+    brain_region_idx = []\\n+    session_info = []\\n+\\n+    for spec in session_specs:\\n+        session = convert_one_session(spec, show_processing=show_processing, outdir=outdir)\\n+        if session is None:\\n+            continue\\n+\\n+        neural.append(session[\\\"neural\\\"])\\n+        inputs.append(session[\\\"input\\\"])\\n+        outputs.append(session[\\\"output\\\"])\\n+        if session[\\\"subject\\\"] not in subject_names:\\n+            subject_names.append(session[\\\"subject\\\"])\\n+        subject_index.append(subject_names.index(session[\\\"subject\\\"]))\\n+        brain_region_idx.append(session[\\\"brain_region_idx\\\"])\\n+        session_info.append(\\n+            {\\n+                \\\"session_id\\\": session[\\\"session_id\\\"],\\n+                \\\"subject\\\": session[\\\"subject\\\"],\\n+                \\\"n_trials\\\": session[\\\"n_trials\\\"],\\n+                \\\"n_units\\\": session[\\\"n_units\\\"],\\n+                \\\"format\\\": session[\\\"format\\\"],\\n+ np.asarray(bp[\\\"hit\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"miss\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"miss\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"no\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"no\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"early\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"early\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"autowater\\\": np.asarray(bp[\\\"autowater\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"stim_enable\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/stim/enable\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            \\\"events\\\": {\\n+                \\\"bitStart\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/bitStart\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"sample\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/sample\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"delay\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/delay\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"goCue\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/goCue\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+                \\\"reward\\\": np.asarray(f[\\\"obj/bp/ev/reward\\\"][()], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1),\\n+            },\\n+            \\\"units\\\": units,\\n+            \\\"traj\\\": traj_views,\\n+            \\\"bitcode_bitstart\\\": bitstart,\\n+            \\\"fs\\\": fs,\\n+            \\\"motion_energy\\\": motion_energy,\\n+            \\\"motion_thresh\\\": motion_thresh,\\n+        }\\n+\\n+\\n+def load_session(spec: SessionSpec) -> dict:\\n+    return load_session_hdf5(spec) if is_hdf5_mat(spec.session_path) else load_session_mat(spec)\\n+\\n+\\n+def good_quality(label: str) -> bool:\\n+    label = label.strip().lower()\\n+    return label not in {\\\"garbage\\\", \\\"gabrga\\\", \\\"noisy\\\", \\\"real?\\\"}\\n+\\n+\\n+def interp_to_taxis(old_t: np.ndarray, values: np.ndarray, new_t: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    old_t = np.asarray(old_t, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    mask = np.isfinite(old_t) & np.isfinite(values)\\n+    if mask.sum() < 2:\\n+        return np.full_like(new_t, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    return np.interp(new_t, old_t[mask], values[mask], left=np.nan, right=np.nan)\\n+\\n+\\n+def find_video_offset(raw: dict) -> float:\\n+    bitstart = np.asarray(raw[\\\"bitcode_bitstart\\\"], dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+    fs = float(raw[\\\"fs\\\"])\\n+    if bitstart.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(fs) or fs <= 0:\\n+        return 0.5\\n+    return robust_mode(bitstart) / fs - robust_mode(raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"bitStart\\\"])\\n+\\n+\\n+def feature_xy(raw: dict, view_idx: int, feature_name: str, align_times: np.ndarray, time_vec: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\n+    trials = raw[\\\"traj\\\"][view_idx]\\n+    taxis = time_vec.copy()\\n+    vidshift = find_video_offset(raw)\\n+    xpos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    ypos = np.full((time_vec.size, len(trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+    for trix, trial in enumerate(trials):\\n+        if not math.isfinite(trial[\\\"n_dropped\\\"]):\\n+            continue\\n+        if feature_name not in trial[\\\"feat_names\\\"]:\\n+            continue\\n+        feat_idx = trial[\\\"feat_names\\\"].index(feature_name)\\n+        ts = np.asarray(trial[\\\"ts\\\"][:, :2, feat_idx], dtype=np.float64)\\n+        if ts.ndim != 2 or ts.shape[0] == 0:\\n+            continue\\n+        frame_times = trial[\\\"frame_times\\\"]\\n+        if frame_times is None or frame_times.size == 0 or np.all(~np.isfinite(frame_times)):\\n+            frame_times = (np.arange(ts.shape[0], dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0\\n+        old_t = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trix]\\n+        xpos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 0], taxis)\\n+        ypos[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, ts[:, 1], taxis)\\n+\\n+        if \\\"tongue\\\" not in feature_name:\\n+            xpos[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(xpos[:, trix])\\n+            ypos[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(ypos[:, trix])\\n+\\n+    return xpos, ypos\\n+\\n+\\n+def feature_speed(xpos: np.ndarray, ypos: np.ndarray, feature_name: str) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    n_time, n_trials = xpos.shape\\n+    xvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    yvel = np.full((n_time, n_trials), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+\\n+    for i in range(n_trials):\\n+        tsinterp = np.column_stack([xpos[:, i], ypos[:, i]])\\n+        basederiv = np.nanmedian(np.diff(tsinterp, axis=0), axis=0) if n_time > 1 else np.array([0.0, 0.0])\\n+        xv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 0])\\n+        yv = np.gradient(tsinterp[:, 1])\\n+        if \\\"tongue\\\" not in feature_name:\\n+            xv = xv - basederiv[0]\\n+            yv = yv - basederiv[0]\\n+            xv = nearest_fill_1d(xv)\\n+            yv = nearest_fill_1d(yv)\\n+        else:\\n+            xv = np.nan_to_num(xv, nan=0.0)\\n+            yv = np.nan_to_num(yv, nan=0.0)\\n+        xvel[:, i] = xv\\n+        yvel[:, i] = yv\\n+    return np.sqrt(xvel**2 + yvel**2)\\n+\\n+\\n+def aligned_motion_energy(raw: dict, align_times: np.ndarray, time_vec: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    me_trials = raw[\\\"motion_energy\\\"]\\n+    side_trials = raw[\\\"traj\\\"][0]\\n+    vidshift = find_video_offset(raw)\\n+    out = np.full((time_vec.size, len(me_trials)), np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    for trix, me in enumerate(me_trials):\\n+        me = np.asarray(me, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)\\n+        if me.size == 0:\\n+            continue\\n+        frame_times = side_trials[trix][\\\"frame_times\\\"] if trix < len(side_trials) else None\\n+        if frame_times is None or frame_times.size == 0 or np.all(~np.isfinite(frame_times)):\\n+            old_t = (np.arange(me.size, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0) / 400.0 - 0.5 - align_times[trix]\\n+        else:\\n+            old_t = frame_times - vidshift - align_times[trix]\\n+        out[:, trix] = interp_to_taxis(old_t, me, time_vec)\\n+        out[:, trix] = nearest_fill_1d(out[:, trix])\\n+    return out\\n+\\n+\\n+def compute_unit_trial_matrix(unit: dict, go_cue: np.ndarray, trial_to_pos: np.ndarray, n_sel: int, time_edges: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    aligned = unit[\\\"trialtm\\\"] - go_cue[unit[\\\"trial\\\"] - 1]\\n+    trial_pos = trial_to_pos[unit[\\\"trial\\\"] - 1]\\n+    keep = (\\n+        (trial_pos >= 0)\\n+        & np.isfinite(aligned)\\n+        & (aligned >= time_edges[0])\\n+        & (aligned < time_edges[-1])\\n+    )\\n+    mat = np.zeros((n_sel, time_edges.size - 1), dtype=np.float64)\\n+    if np.any(keep):\\n+        bins = np.floor((aligned[keep] - time_edges[0]) / DT).astype(np.int64)\\n+        np.add.at(mat, (trial_pos[keep], bins), 1.0 / DT)\\n+    mat = my_smooth(mat.T, SMOOTH, BCTYPE).T\\n+    return mat\\n+\\n+\\n+def summarize_threshold(values: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n+    values = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    values = values[np.isfinite(values)]\\n+    if values.size == 0:\\n+        return 0.0\\n+    return float(np.nanpercentile(values, 50.0))\\n+\\n+\\n+def session_valid_trial_mask(raw: dict) -> np.ndarray:\\n+    return (\\n+        (raw[\\\"stim_enable\\\"] == 0)\\n+        & (raw[\\\"early\\\"] == 0)\\n+        & ((raw[\\\"hit\\\"] == 1) | (raw[\\\"miss\\\"] == 1))\\n+        & ((raw[\\\"R\\\"] == 1) | (raw[\\\"L\\\"] == 1))\\n+    )\\n+\\n+\\n+def plot_processing(session_id: str, time_vec: np.ndarray, session_out: dict, outdir: Path) -> None:\\n+    if not session_out[\\\"neural\\\"]:\\n+        return\\n+    trial_idx = 0\\n+    neural = session_out[\\\"neural\\\"][trial_idx]\\n+    cont = session_out[\\\"continuous\\\"]\\n+    binary = session_out[\\\"output\\\"][trial_idx]\\n+\\n+    fig, axes = plt.subplots(3, 2, figsize=(16, 11))\\n+    ax = axes[0, 0]\\n+    nshow = min(40, neural.shape[0])\\n+    ax.imshow(neural[:nshow], aspect=\\\"auto\\\", cmap=\\\"viridis\\\", extent=[time_vec[0], time_vec[-1], nshow, 0])\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Neural activity (sample trial)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylabel(\\\"Neuron\\\")\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[0, 1]\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, session_out[\\\"input\\\"][trial_idx][0], color=\\\"black\\\", lw=1.5)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Decoder input\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylabel(\\\"Seconds\\\")\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[1, 0]\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"tongue\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"tongue @ {cont['tongue_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"paw\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"paw @ {cont['paw_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.hist(cont[\\\"motion\\\"].reshape(-1), bins=60, alpha=0.5, label=f\\\"motion @ {cont['motion_thr']:.3f}\\\")\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Session thresholds\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[1, 1]\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"tongue\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"tongue\\\")\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"paw\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"paw\\\")\\n+    ax.plot(time_vec, cont[\\\"motion\\\"][:, trial_idx], label=\\\"motion\\\")\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Continuous outputs (sample trial)\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[2, 0]\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[3], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"tongue_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[4], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"paw_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[5], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"motion_bin\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylim(-0.2, 1.2)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Discretized continuous outputs\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    ax = axes[2, 1]\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[0], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"lick_direction\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[1], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"context\\\")\\n+    ax.step(time_vec, binary[2], where=\\\"mid\\\", label=\\\"outcome\\\")\\n+    ax.set_ylim(-0.2, 1.2)\\n+    ax.set_title(\\\"Trial labels as time series\\\")\\n+    ax.set_xlabel(\\\"Time from go cue (s)\\\")\\n+    ax.legend(fontsize=8)\\n+\\n+    fig.suptitle(session_id)\\n+    fig.tight_layout()\\n+    fig.savefig(outdir / f\\\"processing_{session_id}.png\\\", dpi=150)\\n+    plt.close(fig)\\n+\\n+\\n+def convert_one_session(spec: SessionSpec, show_processing: bool, outdir: Path) -> dict | None:\\n+    t0 = time.time()\\n+    raw = load_session(spec)\\n+    valid = session_valid_trial_mask(raw)\\n+    selected_trials = np.flatnonzero(valid)\\n+    if selected_trials.size < 2:\\n+        log(f\\\"SKIP {spec.session_id}: only {selected_trials.size} valid hit/miss non-stim non-early trials\\\")\\n+        return None\\n+\\n+    time_edges = np.arange(TMIN, TMAX + DT, DT, dtype=np.float64)\\n+    time_vec = time_edges[:-1] + DT / 2.0\\n+\\n+    trial_to_pos = np.full(raw[\\\"R\\\"].size, -1, dtype=np.int64)\\n+    trial_to_pos[selected_trials] = np.arange(selected_trials.size, dtype=np.int64)\\n+\\n+    kept_units = 0\\n+    neural_trials = [[] for _ in range(selected_trials.size)]\\n+    for unit in raw[\\\"units\\\"]:\\n+        if not good_quality(unit[\\\"quality\\\"]):\\n+            continue\\n+        unit_mat = compute_unit_trial_matrix(unit, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], trial_to_pos, selected_trials.size, time_edges)\\n+        if float(unit_mat.mean()) <= LOW_FR_HZ:\\n+            continue\\n+        kept_units += 1\\n+        unit_mat = unit_mat.astype(np.float32)\\n+        for trix in range(selected_trials.size):\\n+            neural_trials[trix].append(unit_mat[trix])\\n+\\n+    if kept_units < 10:\\n+        log(f\\\"SKIP {spec.session_id}: only {kept_units} units after quality/FR filtering\\\")\\n+        return None\\n+\\n+    tongue_pos = feature_xy(raw, 0, \\\"tongue\\\", raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+    tongue_speed = feature_speed(*tongue_pos, \\\"tongue\\\")\\n+\\n+    paw_speeds = []\\n+    for paw_name in (\\\"top_paw\\\", \\\"bottom_paw\\\"):\\n+        paw_pos = feature_xy(raw, 1, paw_name, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+        paw_speeds.append(feature_speed(*paw_pos, paw_name))\\n+    paw_speed = np.nanmean(np.stack(paw_speeds, axis=0), axis=0)\\n+    paw_speed = np.nan_to_num(paw_speed, nan=0.0)\\n+\\n+    motion = aligned_motion_energy(raw, raw[\\\"events\\\"][\\\"goCue\\\"], time_vec)\\n+    motion = np.nan_to_num(motion, nan=0.0)\\n+\\n+    tongue_sel = tongue_speed[:, selected_trials]\\n+    paw_sel = paw_speed[:, selected_trials]\\n+    motion_sel = motion[:, selected_trials]\\n+\\n+    tongue_thr = summarize_threshold(tongue_sel)\\n+    paw_thr = summarize_threshold(paw_sel)\\n+    motion_thr = summarize_threshold(motion_sel)\\n+\\n+    input_trials = []\\n+    output_trials = []\\n+    final_neural = []\\n+    for local_idx, trial_idx in enumerate(selected_trials):\\n+        neural_arr = np.stack(neural_trials[local_idx], axis=0).astype(np.float32)\\n+        final_neural.append(neural_arr)\\n+        input_trials.append(time_vec[None, :].astype(np.float32))\\n+\\n+        lick_direction = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"R\\\"][trial_idx] == 1 else 0)\\n+        context = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"autowater\\\"][trial_idx] == 0 else 0)\\n+        outcome = np.int64(1 if raw[\\\"hit\\\"][trial_idx] == 1 else 0)\\n+        output_arr = np.vstack(\\n+            [\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, lick_direction, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, context, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                np.full(time_vec.size, outcome, dtype=np.int64),\\n+                (tongue_sel[:, local_idx] >= tongue_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+                (paw_sel[:, local_idx] >= paw_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+                (motion_sel[:, local_idx] >= motion_thr).astype(np.int64),\\n+            ]\\n+        )\\n+        output_trials.append(output_arr)\\n+\\n+    session_out = {\\n+        \\\"session_id\\\": spec.session_id,\\n+        \\\"subject\\\": spec.subject,\\n+        \\\"neural\\\": final_neural,\\n+        \\\"input\\\": input_trials,\\n+        \\\"output\\\": output_trials,\\n+        \\\"brain_region_idx\\\": np.zeros((kept_units,), dtype=np.int64),\\n+        \\\"n_trials\\\": len(final_neural),\\n+        \\\"n_units\\\": kept_units,\\n+        \\\"format\\\": raw[\\\"format\\\"],\\n+        \\\"source_file\\\": str(spec.session_path),\\n+        \\\"continuous\\\": {\\n+            \\\"tongue\\\": tongue_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"paw\\\": paw_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"motion\\\": motion_sel.astype(np.float32),\\n+            \\\"tongue_thr\\\": tongue_thr,\\n+            \\\"paw_thr\\\": paw_thr,\\n+            \\\"motion_thr\\\": motion_thr,\\n+        },\\n+    }\\n+    if show_processing:\\n+        plot_processing(spec.session_id, time_vec, session_out, outdir)\\n+\\n+    log(\\n+        f\\\"SESSION {spec.session_id}: kept {session_out['n_trials']} trials, \\\"\\n+        f\\\"{session_out['n_units']} units, format={raw['format']}, \\\"\\n+        f\\\"time={time.time() - t0:.2f}s\\\"\\n+    )\\n+    return session_out\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_dataset(session_specs: list[SessionSpec], show_processing: bool, outdir: Path) -> dict:\\n+    neural = []\\n+    inputs = []\\n+    outputs = []\\n+    subject_names: list[str] = []\\n+    subject_index = []\\n+    brain_region_idx = []\\n+    session_info = []\\n+\\n+    for spec in session_specs:\\n+        session = convert_one_session(spec, show_processing=show_processing, outdir=outdir)\\n+        if session is None:\\n+            continue\\n+\\n+        neural.append(session[\\\"neural\\\"])\\n+        inputs.append(session[\\\"input\\\"])\\n+        outputs.append(session[\\\"output\\\"])\\n+        if session[\\\"subject\\\"] not in subject_names:\\n+            subject_names.append(session[\\\"subject\\\"])\\n+        subject_index.append(subject_names.index(session[\\\"subject\\\"]))\\n+        brain_region_idx.append(session[\\\"brain_region_idx\\\"])\\n+        session_info.append(\\n+            {\\n+                \\\"session_id\\\": session[\\\"session_id\\\"],\\n+                \\\"subject\\\": session[\\\"subject\\\"],\\n+                \\\"n_trials\\\": session[\\\"n_trials\\\"],\\n+                \\\"n_units\\\": session[\\\"n_units\\\"],\\n+                \\\"format\\\": session[\\\"format\\\"],\\n+                \\\"source_file\\\": session[\\\"source_file\\\"],\\n+            }\\n+        )\\n+\\n+    data = {\\n+        \\\"neural\\\": neural,\\n+        \\\"input\\\": inputs,\\n+        \\\"output\\\": outputs,\\n+        \\\"subjects\\\": subject_names,\\n+        \\\"subject_idx\\\": np.asarray(subject_index, dtype=np.int64),\\n+        \\\"brain_regions\\\": [\\\"ALM\\\"],\\n+        \\\"brain_region_idx\\\": brain_region_idx,\\n+        \\\"input_names\\\": [\\\"time_from_go_cue_s\\\"],\\n+        \\\"output_names\\\": [\\n+            \\\"lick_direction\\\",\\n+            \\\"behavioral_context\\\",\\n+            \\\"outcome\\\",\\n+            \\\"tongue_velocity_bin\\\",\\n+            \\\"paw_velocity_bin\\\",\\n+            \\\"motion_energy_bin\\\",\\n+        ],\\n+        \\\"output_values\\\": [\\n+            [\\\"left\\\", \\\"right\\\"],\\n+            [\\\"WC\\\", \\\"DR\\\"],\\n+            [\\\"incorrect\\\", \\\"correct\\\"],\\n+            [\\\"below_session_median\\\", \\\"at_or_above_session_median\\\"],\\n+            [\\\"below_session_median\\\", \\\"at_or_above_session_median\\\"],\\n+            [\\\"below_session_median\\\", \\\"at_or_above_session_median\\\"],\\n+        ],\\n+        \\\"metadata\\\": {\\n+            \\\"task_description\\\": (\\n+                \\\"ALM spike-rate decoding aligned to go cue, predicting lick direction, \\\"\\n+                \\\"behavioral context, outcome, and binarized tongue/paw/motion variables.\\\"\\n+            ),\\n+            \\\"time_bin_size\\\": float(DT * 1000.0),\\n+            \\\"temporal_alignment_event\\\": \\\"go cue onset (stored bp.ev.goCue for every trial)\\\",\\n+            \\\"off_start\\\": float(TMIN),\\n+            \\\"off_end\\\": float(TMAX),\\n+            \\\"neural_processing\\\": \\\"Selected ALM probe, bad-label exclusion, goCue alignment, 5 ms binning, causal Gaussian smoothing, FR > 1 Hz\\\",\\n+            \\\"trial_filter\\\": \\\"~stim.enable & ~early & (hit | miss)\\\",\\n+            \\\"session_selection\\\": \\\"Reference code ALM session list intersected with available data files\\\",\\n+            \\\"video_processing\\\": \\\"Reference-style interpolation to neural time base with video offset correction\\\",\\n+            \\\"session_info\\\": session_info,\\n+        },\\n+    }\\n+    return data\\n+\\n+\\n+def print_summary(data: dict) -> None:\\n+    n_sessions = len(data[\\\"neural\\\"])\\n+    n_trials = sum(len(s) for s in data[\\\"neural\\\"])\\n+    n_units = sum(s[0].shape[0] for s in data[\\\"neural\\\"]) if n_sessions else 0\\n+    log(f\\\"SUMMARY sessions={n_sessions} trials={n_trials} total_units={n_units}\\\")\\n+    if n_sessions:\\n+        trial_counts = [len(s) for s in data[\\\"neural\\\"]]\\n+        unit_counts = [s[0].shape[0] for s in data[\\\"neural\\\"]]\\n+        log(\\n+            \\\"SUMMARY trials/session \\\"\\n+            f\\\"min={min(trial_counts)} mean={np.mean(trial_counts):.2f} max={max(trial_counts)}\\\"\\n+        )\\n+        log(\\n+            \\\"SUMMARY units/session \\\"\\n+            f\\\"min={min(unit_counts)} mean={np.mean(unit_counts):.2f} max={max(unit_counts)}\\\"\\n+        )\\n+\\n+\\n+def main() -> None:\\n+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"Convert neural decoder dataset.\\\")\\n+    parser.add_argument(\\\"outpicklefile\\\", type=str, help=\\\"Output pickle path\\\")\\n+    mode = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\\n+    mode.add_argument(\\\"--full\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Process all reference-selected sessions (default)\\\")\\n+    mode.add_argument(\\\"--sample\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Process only 2 sessions\\\")\\n+    parser.add_argument(\\\"--show-processing\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Save processing plots for converted sessions\\\")\\n+    args = parser.parse_args()\\n+\\n+    root = Path(\\\".\\\").resolve()\\n+    code_dir = root / \\\"code\\\"\\n+    data_dir = root / \\\"data\\\"\\n+    out_path = Path(args.outpicklefile).resolve()\\n+\\n+ resolve which probe to use |\\n\\n### Key Decisions\\n1. **Retain only the 44 code-selected ALM sessions present in `data/`**: This is the cleanest way to match the paper\\u2019s analyzed dataset rather than the broader raw archive.\\n2. **Use the ALM-designated probe only for each retained session**: Raw files can contain multiple probes/areas; the reference loaders specify which probe corresponds to ALM.\\n3. **Exclude stimulation, early-lick, and ignore/no-response trials**: Reference analyses consistently use `~stim.enable`, `~early`, and usually hit/miss conditions; ignore trials are omitted in the paper.\\n4. **Use `goCue` as the universal alignment event**: This matches the user request and the default reference alignment.\\n5. **Use a fixed window of `[-2.5, 2.5] s` around `goCue` with a 5 ms bin (`dt = 1/200`)**: This matches the default reference processing pipeline better than the 10 ms tutorial example and gives a shared time axis for neural/video streams.\\n6. **Use all non-garbage manually curated units on the selected probe, then apply a 1 Hz FR threshold**: This matches the paper\\u2019s \\u201call units >1 Hz\\u201d rule for most analyses better than a single-unit-only subset.\\n7. **Represent trial-level categorical outputs as constant time series**: This keeps all outputs on the same `(n_output, n_timepoints)` grid while preserving the per-trial labels requested by the user.\\n8. **Define tongue velocity from the side-view `tongue` marker speed magnitude**: This uses a direct reference-processed kinematic channel without inventing an unreferenced cross-camera combination.\\n9. **Define paw velocity from the average of top- and bottom-paw speed magnitudes in the bottom view**: This captures overall paw movement while remaining close to the tracked features described in the paper/code.\\n10. **Use per-session 50th-percentile thresholds for tongue velocity, paw velocity, and motion energy**: This follows the decoder task even where it differs from the paper\\u2019s manual movement threshold.\\n\\n### Planned Sanity Checks\\n- [ ] Verify that retained session list exactly matches the 44-session intersection of reference loader scripts and available data files.\\n- [ ] Compare retained fixed-delay and randomized-delay session counts against the paper\\u2019s 25 and 19 session totals.\\n- [ ] After probe selection and 1 Hz filtering, compare unit counts against the paper\\u2019s reported 1,651 fixed-delay and 845 randomized-delay units.\\n- [ ] Spot-check one neuron in one trial by histogramming raw spike times directly from the source file and comparing to the converted binned/smoothed trace.\\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial\\u2019s `lick_direction`, `context`, and `outcome` labels directly from raw `bp` fields using `np.allclose()`.\\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial\\u2019s aligned motion energy against direct interpolation from the raw motion-energy file using the source frame times / video offset logic.\\n- [ ] Spot-check one trial\\u2019s aligned tongue and paw velocity traces against direct interpolation of raw DLC coordinates followed by the same velocity computation.\\n- [ ] Check that all trials in the converted dataset share the same time vector and that `input[0]` matches the neural bin centers exactly.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 6: Script Development\\n**Status**: COMPLETE\\n\\n[Implementation notes]\\n- Created `convert_data.py`.\\n- Script design:\\n  - Parses the reference ALM session loader scripts to recover the analyzed session list and probe selections.\\n  - Supports both MATLAB v7.3/HDF5 session files (`h5py`) and older MATLAB session files (`scipy.io.loadmat`).\\n  - Reimplements the reference spike binning and smoothing path in Python, including the causal Gaussian kernel behavior in `mySmooth.m`.\\n  - Loads and aligns motion energy and selected DLC features to the neural time base using the reference video-offset logic.\\n  - Filters to non-stim, non-early hit/miss trials and builds decoder-ready `input`/`output` arrays.\\n  - Supports `--full`, `--sample`, and `--show-processing`.\\n\\nCode inefficiencies identified:\\n- Full recursive HDF5-to-Python loading was too slow and memory-heavy for the large session files.\\n- Per-spike/per-trial histogram loops would likely be a bottleneck if implemented naively.\\n\\nCode speedups added:\\n- Implemented targeted field loading instead of full-session recursive decoding for HDF5 files.\\n- Implemented vectorized per-unit spike accumulation with `np.add.at` instead of calling a histogram inside nested trial loops.\\n- Processes one session at a time to keep memory bounded.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and
